| `COMM-403` | The 95%-to-99%+ coverage jump costs disproportionately in a constellation too: core fleets deliver ~90-95% and full coverage requires "adding satellites at polar inclinations" or "another plane" to chase the empty edges; same geometric cause (sparse, high-latitude, low-density ground) in satellite and terrestrial media. | `derived_estimate` | The last-percent disproportionate cost | [direct_communication/dtc_coverage_geography.md](direct_communication/dtc_coverage_geography.md) Section 4.2; CBO Large Constellations, Portillo et al., Nature Communications | FACT/DERIVED, multi-source. |
| `COMM-404` | Target-shape consequence: a 95%-of-population D2C target is a SINGLE inclined ~53-deg band sized to the SOC FLOOR (the few-hundred-satellite US+Europe-equivalent band, COMM-215; near-global mid-latitude ~290-960, COMM-216), no polar shells, because covering the 53-deg band IS covering ~95% of people; chasing the last few percent adds polar shells and the 2-4x multi-fold multiplier (a separately-priced scope decision). | `derived_estimate` | The 95%-target = one-band-at-the-floor conclusion (load-bearing) | [direct_communication/dtc_coverage_geography.md](direct_communication/dtc_coverage_geography.md) Section 4.2; cross-ref COMM-215, COMM-216, COMM-225 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-405` | Coverage-geography counterpart to the coverage-vs-capacity finding (COMM-224): coverage of where the people are is cheap (one ~53-deg band at the floor); the expensive marginal satellites go to the sparse high-latitude edges (the last few percent of people) and to capacity over dense ground, not to the bulk of the population. | `derived_estimate` | The where-the-dollars-go restatement | [direct_communication/dtc_coverage_geography.md](direct_communication/dtc_coverage_geography.md) Section 4; cross-ref COMM-224 | DERIVED. |

## COMM - Communications Claims (Wave 7: the DTC supply side, the spectrum-capacity physics, spectrum access, the rate-vs-spectrum sweep, subscribers-per-satellite, and the ground cost-per-subscriber)

This continues the master reconciliation for communications wave 7 (ingested
2026-06-23 to 2026-06-26), the direct-to-cell supply-side wave: the governing DTC
system model landed (its block `COMM-315`..`COMM-335` + `COMM-356`..`COMM-365` is
now expanded to the full per-claim ledger in the wave-6 section above), plus six
new source docs that quantify the supply side. Each doc mints its own contiguous
in-doc `COMM-###` block, and the in-doc IDs equal these global IDs. Allocation:
per-satellite capacity supply `COMM-406`..`COMM-425`; the spectrum-capacity primer
`COMM-426`..`COMM-439`; the DTC spectrum-access doc `COMM-481`..`COMM-492`; the
rate-vs-spectrum doc `COMM-493`..`COMM-512`; the ground cost-per-subscriber doc
`COMM-521`..`COMM-534`; the subscribers-per-satellite doc `COMM-535`..`COMM-560`.
Several blocks deliberately leave a reserved-unused tail: `COMM-440`..`COMM-450`
(primer), `COMM-451`..`COMM-480` (declared by the spectrum-access doc, which mints
only `COMM-481`..`COMM-492`), `COMM-513`..`COMM-520` (rate-vs-spectrum). The
`channels_aggregate_answer.md` one-page companion mints NO IDs of its own; it
cross-references the primer's `COMM-427`/`COMM-428`/`COMM-433`/`COMM-434`. The
`ground_cellular_cost_per_subscriber.md` doc cites its carried inputs as
`global COMM-NNN` (the global SOURCE_INDEX namespace, e.g. global COMM-091/100/172),
a separate namespace from its own new `COMM-521`..`COMM-534`. China is excluded
where the doc states a geographic scope (spectrum-access and ground-cost docs
explicitly; the physics docs do not discuss it).

### Per-satellite capacity supply (COMM-406..425)

| Claim ID | Claim text | Source status | Role | Links or internal references | Uncertainty notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `COMM-406` | Per-satellite total DTC capacity = N_beams x C_cell x f_reuse, bounded above by min(C_spectrum, C_processor), where C_cell = owned bandwidth x spectral efficiency (Shannon) and C_spectrum = owned bandwidth x SE x the number of non-overlapping reuse beams over the footprint; the supply-side identity assembled from the corpus's Shannon-x-beams framework. | `derived_estimate` | The supply-side capacity identity | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-108, COMM-224, COMM-114, COMM-344, COMM-141/142 | DERIVED, framework. |
| `COMM-407` | The number of simultaneous beams a phased array forms scales with aperture area / element count (aperture is inversely related to beam angular size, independent-beam count tracks element count); AST's ~223 m^2 array forms ~2,500 beams. | `certified` | Phased-array beam-count physics | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); USPTO 12200508, Kilic Radio Science 2009, arXiv 2506.18672 | FACT. |
| `COMM-408` | A flat ~20-24 m^2 array forms an estimated ~200-450 beams (central ~250 by linear-in-area scaling from AST's 2,500 at 223 m^2), but Starlink's flying ~25 m^2-class D2C array forms only 48 beams, a ~5x spread driven by the unpublished onboard digital-beamforming processor size; this is the SOFTEST, UNKNOWN-grade input in the chain. | `derived_estimate` | The flat-array beam count (softest input) | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); arXiv 2506.18672, thexlab.org, cross-ref COMM-270 | DERIVED + UNKNOWN (~5x spread). |
| `COMM-409` | Per-cell capacity on ~25 MHz of owned spectrum is ~50-75 Mbps (25 MHz x ~2-3 bps/Hz), using the corpus's data-grade DTC spectral-efficiency band (AST ~3 bps/Hz; Starlink measured ~0.52-0.61 bps/Hz at ~0 dB SINR; 4G ~1.5 bps/Hz). | `derived_estimate` | Per-cell capacity on 25 MHz | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); arXiv 2506.18672, arXiv 2506.00283, cross-ref COMM-114, COMM-344 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-410` | A flat ~20-24 m^2 array on ~25 MHz produces ~5-15 Gbps total per-satellite DTC throughput (central ~8-10 Gbps), the realistic spectrum-and-reuse-bound figure; the raw beam-times-cell product (~10-34 Gbps) is the optimistic bound that double-counts the shared spectrum. This brackets Starlink V2-mini D2C (~7 Gbps) and sits far below AST Block 2 (~56 Gbps, 10x aperture, 8x spectrum). | `derived_estimate` | The per-satellite total throughput | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); cross-ref COMM-142, thexlab.org / NextBigFuture | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-411` | On ~25 MHz of owned spectrum the binding cap is the SPECTRUM, not the onboard processor: 25 MHz x 3 bps/Hz x even ~50x reuse is ~3.75 Gbps from one slice, so reaching ~10-15 Gbps needs ~130-200x effective reuse, while the AST processor cap is ~10 GHz processing / ~56 Gbps, far above what 25 MHz can feed; AST's own analysis says it operates near the practical limits of antenna size and power so expanded bandwidth is the most viable path to more throughput. | `derived_estimate` | Spectrum binds, not the processor | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); arXiv 2506.18672, COMM-141/142, cross-ref COMM-325 | DERIVED + FACT. |
| `COMM-412` | V2-mini D2C ~7 Gbps aggregate (48 beams x ~7 Mbps, fronthaul-limited) and AST Block 2 ~56 Gbps theoretical (2,800 cells x 20 Mbps, 10 GHz processing) are the lower and upper per-satellite anchors; the V3 D2C ~700 Gbps projection is a dedicated-15,000-sat-fleet projection with much more spectrum and processor, NOT a flat-entrant anchor. | `sourced_estimate` | The lower/upper per-satellite anchors | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); thexlab.org, NextBigFuture, COMM-142 | FACT lower/upper; the 700 Gbps is a projection. |
| `COMM-413` | THE SPECTRUM-SATURATION CEILING (formula): over a fixed ground area on a fixed owned bandwidth, max area capacity = B_owned x SE x N_reuse, where N_reuse = A_ground / A_beam is the number of NON-OVERLAPPING co-channel beams that tile the area, set by beam footprint (aperture/altitude), NOT by satellite count. | `derived_estimate` | The saturation-ceiling formula | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-224, COMM-108 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-414` | THE SATURATION MECHANISM (multi-source): once every patch of the target is covered by one beam on the owned spectrum, adding more satellites over the SAME area adds OVERLAPPING co-channel beams that interfere rather than add capacity, so per-user speed stops rising (interference-limited): full frequency reuse "leads to severe inter-beam co-channel interference and degrades the SINR," and "overlaps of multiple co-channel beams can reduce the communication capacity." | `certified` | The interference mechanism | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); IEEE Xplore 10816533, arXiv 2501.02750, arXiv 2310.15011, MDPI Electronics 13/6/1096, cross-ref COMM-224 | FACT, multi-source. |
| `COMM-415` | This is the founder's "past some point more satellites stop helping speed" point, and it is correct: per-user speed is capped at (B_owned x SE) / users_active regardless of satellite count; satellite count helps only until the target is tiled by one non-overlapping beam on the owned spectrum (the coverage floor, ~130-450 sats for US+Europe), and past the reuse limit more same-aperture satellites add cost without raising per-user speed. | `derived_estimate` | The founder's saturation point confirmed | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); COMM-215/224, COMM-414 | DERIVED + FACT. |
| `COMM-416` | The saturation ceiling moves ONLY with (a) more owned spectrum (linear, the strongest lever and AST's stated path), (b) higher spectral efficiency (bounded by the ~0 dB-SINR phone link, ~2-4x headroom), or (c) more non-overlapping beams via smaller footprints (bigger aperture or lower altitude); adding satellites of the SAME aperture on the SAME spectrum over the SAME area is none of these and hits the ceiling. | `derived_estimate` | The three levers that raise the ceiling | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-320, COMM-325, COMM-226, COMM-411 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-417` | THE SPEED-vs-USERS FORMULA: sustained per-user speed = C_cell / users_active = (B_owned x SE) / users_active, where users_active is the simultaneously-transmitting users in the beam (a concurrency fraction of attached phones), because the cell's spectrum pool is time-shared by the scheduler across active users. | `derived_estimate` | The speed-vs-users formula | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-336..340 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-418` | Worked speed-vs-users on a ~50-75 Mbps cell (25 MHz x 2-3 bps/Hz): 1 active user ~62.5 Mbps, 2-3 ~21-31 Mbps (the founder's ~20-30 Mbps target), 5 ~12.5, 10 ~6.25, 50 ~1.25, 125 ~0.5 Mbps; the 125-user / ~500 kbps row matches AST's published ~500 kbps/user rural figure and the 50-user / ~1 Mbps row matches the analyst busy-cell figure, calibrating the formula to both flying datapoints. | `derived_estimate` | The worked speed-vs-users table (cross-checked) | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); arXiv 2506.18672, cross-ref COMM-339 | DERIVED, cross-checked to two datapoints. |
| `COMM-419` | To DELIVER ~20-30 Mbps sustained per user the cell must hold active users to ~2-4 (62.5 / 25 = 2.5); the number of users served at the target across a fleet = (total non-overlapping beams over the area) x (~2-4 users/beam). So the ~20-30 Mbps headline is a low-concurrency / lightly-loaded number, and serving more users at the target requires MORE BEAMS (bigger aperture or more spectrum), not more satellites past the tiling limit. | `derived_estimate` | The ~20-30 Mbps is a low-concurrency headline | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-417, COMM-416 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-420` | A ~324 km^2 beam over populated ground holds far more than 2-4 ATTACHED phones; it holds ~2-4 ACTIVE (the ~20-30 Mbps condition) only at low concurrency (~0.5-2% busy-hour activity factor), so it can carry ~100-800 attached users while keeping ~2-4 active, and the ~20-30 Mbps degrades toward ~1 Mbps when concurrency spikes (event/crowd/outage). | `derived_estimate` | Attached vs active in a beam (concurrency) | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-417, COMM-418, arXiv 2506.18672 | DERIVED, concurrency-dependent. |
| `COMM-421` | The populated-band ACTIVE-USER DENSITY and concurrency factor are an UNKNOWN, named gap: the corpus has no grounded active-users-per-km^2 for a target band, which is the input that turns the speed-vs-users formula into a fleet sizing; the founder must set the assumed concurrency and active-user density. | `placeholder` | The active-user-density gap | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md) Section 4.3 / 6 | UNKNOWN, founder assumption. |
| `COMM-422` | The supply ceiling confirms WHY the founder's "much cheaper" framing is the only door: the same 25 MHz a satellite beam spends once over ~324 km^2 is spent ~100+ times by terrestrial cell-splitting in that area, so DTC area capacity is ~30x-to-thousands-x below terrestrial and DTC delivery is ~$5-9/GB vs ~$0.30/GB terrestrial; the satellite wins only on COVERAGE and must price ~20-30 Mbps far below terrestrial's ~170-180 Mbps / ~$80/mo. | `derived_estimate` | Why the cheaper-coverage framing is the only door | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-108, COMM-114, COMM-147 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-423` | The fewest-satellites-for-best-speed answer: speed to one lightly-loaded phone is set by aperture + owned spectrum + altitude (~25-50 Mbps) and is NOT raised by adding satellites; satellites buy coverage up to the floor and capacity (more users) up to the reuse ceiling, so the fewest satellites that clear the coverage floor (a few hundred for US+Europe) already deliver the best per-user speed the spectrum allows, and the lever for more served users or higher speed is spectrum and aperture, not airframe count. | `derived_estimate` | The fewest-satellites-for-best-speed answer | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-361, COMM-415, COMM-419, COMM-215/224 | DERIVED, synthesis. |
| `COMM-424` | The spatial-reuse fraction (f_reuse) for a flat-array constellation is an ESTIMATE: satellite reuse factors span ~3-4 to ~20x by isolation, and the ~30-60%-of-beams-co-channel assumption is a planning band, not a measured value for this aperture/altitude; it is one of the swing inputs on the ~5-15 Gbps per-satellite total. | `sourced_estimate` | The spatial-reuse-fraction estimate | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); cross-ref rf_limited_service | ESTIMATE, a swing input. |
| `COMM-425` | Net supply-side picture for a Neutron DTC business: a flat ~20-24 m^2 array on ~25 MHz produces ~5-15 Gbps/sat (spectrum-bound, not processor-bound); the spectrum-saturation ceiling caps per-user speed at (25 MHz x SE) / users_active regardless of satellite count; ~20-30 Mbps sustained needs ~2-4 active users/cell (a low-concurrency headline); the levers that raise the ceiling are owned spectrum and aperture, not airframe count; and the ~25 MHz here is well below the ~100-200 MHz competitive benchmark, the binding constraint on the whole supply side. No verdict. | `derived_estimate` | The net supply-side picture | [direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md](direct_communication/dtc_capacity_supply.md); grounds in COMM-410, COMM-413, COMM-415, COMM-419, COMM-325 | DERIVED/SYNTHESIS. No verdict. |

### Spectrum-capacity primer (COMM-426..439; COMM-440..450 reserved-unused)

| Claim ID | Claim text | Source status | Role | Links or internal references | Uncertainty notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `COMM-426` | Frequency (band, MHz/GHz) sets reach, channel bandwidth (slice width, MHz) sets the capacity ceiling, and data rate (Mbps) is bandwidth times a link-dependent efficiency; they are three distinct quantities, two quoted in MHz, and low frequency reaches better because path loss rises with frequency (FSPL = 20 log10(d) + 20 log10(f) + 92.45). | `certified` | Band vs bandwidth vs data rate | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); Wikipedia Free-space path loss, Waveform, cross-ref COMM-294 | FACT, textbook. |
| `COMM-427` | Shannon-Hartley C = B x log2(1+SNR) (C bits/sec, B Hz, SNR linear) sets the hard max error-free rate; rate is linear in bandwidth and only logarithmic in SNR; real systems reach ~60-80%. | `certified` | The Shannon-Hartley capacity law | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); Wikipedia Shannon-Hartley, RF Essentials, cross-ref COMM-041/315 | FACT, textbook. |
| `COMM-428` | Spectral efficiency = C/B = log2(1+SNR) bps/Hz depends only on link quality; real values LTE ~1.5 avg (peak ~15), 5G ~3 real/stream (peak ~23-30), DTC ~0.5-0.8 measured (Starlink) to ~3 claimed (AST). | `certified` | Spectral-efficiency reference values | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); Techplayon, Waveform, arXiv 2312.00957, cross-ref COMM-114/409 | FACT, multi-source. |
| `COMM-429` | Satellite-to-phone spectral efficiency is low (~0.5-0.8 bps/Hz Starlink-measured) because the phone is a weak terminal (~0.2 W into ~0 dBi) far away; the live network shows MEASURED median SINR 0 dB (SNR=1, ~1 bps/Hz natural ceiling). Most-cited measurement reports mean 0.79 / median 0.64; SMS-phase 0.52-0.61 is the same service at a lower reading; honest band ~0.5-0.8. | `certified` | Why DTC spectral efficiency is low (measured) | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); arXiv 2506.00283, cross-ref COMM-293/294/337 | FACT, measured; spread flagged. |
| `COMM-430` | "25 MHz -> 75 Mbps" is NOT a hard cap; it is rate = bandwidth x spectral efficiency at ~3 bps/Hz (25 x 3 = 75), the AST-claimed end; higher SNR yields more, lower DTC efficiency yields less. | `derived_estimate` | The "25 MHz -> 75 Mbps" is not a cap | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); arXiv 2506.18672, cross-ref COMM-344/409 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-431` | Honest per-cell range on 25 MHz for a real phone link: ~13-20 Mbps at ~0.5-0.8 bps/Hz (Starlink measured), ~37.5 at 1.5, ~50 at 2, ~75 at 3 (AST claimed); efficiency is the entire swing and 75 Mbps is the optimistic top, not a ceiling or floor. | `derived_estimate` | The honest per-cell range on 25 MHz | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); arXiv 2506.00283, arXiv 2506.18672, cross-ref COMM-409/418 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-432` | The 75 Mbps figure is PER CELL (single-phone-alone peak), not per user under load, and assumes 25 MHz cleanly owned; Starlink's measured service is 2x5 MHz shared PCS, hence single-digit Mbps beams; asserting 75 Mbps as a fixed ceiling is wrong in both directions. | `derived_estimate` | Corrects the 75 Mbps framing | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); cross-ref COMM-337/418, arXiv 2506.00283 | DERIVED, corrects prior framing. |
| `COMM-433` | Carrier aggregation (LTE-A Release 10+) lets ONE user receive/transmit on multiple component carriers simultaneously with rates SUMMING, up to 5 carriers / 100 MHz LTE-A (more in 5G); within a carrier the scheduler can assign one user many resource blocks, so a lightly loaded cell's single-device speed equals the full per-beam capacity; ceiling is total bandwidth. | `certified` | Carrier aggregation sums channels | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); 3GPP Carrier Aggregation, Wikipedia Carrier aggregation, 3GLTEInfo, cross-ref COMM-340 | FACT, standardized. |
| `COMM-434` | OFDM splits a channel into orthogonal subcarriers and OFDMA assigns subsets (resource blocks) to different users simultaneously: a MULTIPLE-ACCESS/sharing mechanism, NOT capacity creation; total bits/sec stays bandwidth x efficiency regardless of slicing. | `certified` | OFDMA shares, does not create capacity | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); TechTarget OFDMA, Mini-Circuits OFDM, cross-ref COMM-340/359/427 | FACT, textbook. |
| `COMM-435` | Total system capacity multiplies by frequency reuse: total = bandwidth x spectral efficiency x number of cells; more cells come from smaller cells (cell splitting), the dominant historical driver of wireless capacity (Cooper's Law). | `certified` | Capacity multiplies by frequency reuse | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); GeeksforGeeks frequency reuse, Cooper's Law, cross-ref COMM-413 | FACT, textbook. |
| `COMM-436` | For a satellite the cell is the beam, and the reuse multiplier (number of non-overlapping co-channel beams tiling an area) is set by beam footprint (aperture/altitude), NOT satellite count (AST ~2,500 beams at 223 m^2 / ~324 km^2; Starlink V2-mini 48); past tiling, more same-aperture satellites add overlapping co-channel beams that interfere rather than add capacity. | `certified` | Satellite reuse is footprint-set, not count-set | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); arXiv 2506.18672, IEEE Xplore 10816533, arXiv 2501.02750, cross-ref COMM-407/414/415 | FACT mechanism, multi-source. |
| `COMM-437` | Antenna gain G = 4 pi eta A / lambda^2 (area A, efficiency ~0.55-0.65, wavelength lambda) rises linearly with area (double area = +3 dB), with an inverse gain-beamwidth relation (bigger aperture = narrower beam = smaller cell), so a bigger antenna raises both SNR and reuse at once. | `certified` | The gain-from-aperture law | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); Wikipedia Parabolic antenna, Electronics Notes, cross-ref COMM-296 | FACT, textbook. |
| `COMM-438` | More gain -> more SNR -> more bits/Hz (efficiency = log2(1+SNR)), so a bigger antenna can effectively use more bandwidth, climbing from the ~0.5-0.8 bps/Hz floor toward ~2-3; sensitivity +1 dB ~ 1.26x rate, +2 dB ~ 1.585x (corpus-corrected from an earlier 1.58x/+1 dB error). | `derived_estimate` | Gain enables higher efficiency (with the dB correction) | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); Wikipedia Shannon-Hartley, cross-ref COMM-349/356 | DERIVED + FACT. |
| `COMM-439` | Aperture's gain ENABLES higher altitude: a higher orbit covers more Earth (fewer satellites) but the longer link is weaker, and only more aperture closes it to a weak phone; altitude is a weak trim (~3.5-3.9 dB, 550->350 km) vs aperture's strong lever (~9.5 dB, 25->223 m^2), which is why AST uses ~223 m^2 to operate at a planned ~725-740 km and still deliver broadband. | `certified` | Aperture enables altitude | [direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md](direct_communication/spectrum_capacity_primer.md); arXiv 2506.18672, cross-ref COMM-316/346/357 | FACT, sourced in corpus. |

> The `channels_aggregate_answer.md` one-page companion to the primer mints NO COMM ids of its own; it is a prose answer cross-referencing the primer's `COMM-427`/`COMM-428`/`COMM-433`/`COMM-434` (carrier aggregation sums separate channels so total capacity is the sum of all held channels; OFDMA only shares one channel). It is catalogued in LIBRARY.md and RESEARCH_TRACKER.md but carries no ledger rows here.

### DTC spectrum access (COMM-481..492; COMM-451..480 reserved-unused)

The spectrum-access doc declares the wider `COMM-451`..`COMM-492` block but mints only `COMM-481`..`COMM-492`; `COMM-451`..`COMM-480` are reserved-unused. China is explicitly excluded.

| Claim ID | Claim text | Source status | Role | Links or internal references | Uncertainty notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `COMM-481` | Aperture and held bandwidth are independent dials: antenna gain G = 4 pi eta A / lambda^2 sets link quality (G/T, EIRP, per-beam rate), NOT how many MHz the operator is licensed to hold; carrier aggregation sums separately-held channels so total capacity tracks total MHz held. The founder's premise (a 25 m^2 antenna does not force a 25 MHz signal) is correct. | `derived_estimate` | Aperture != bandwidth | [direct_communication/dtc_spectrum_access.md](direct_communication/dtc_spectrum_access.md) Section 1.1; cross-ref COMM-313, channels_aggregate_answer.md | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-482` | But the unmodified phone re-couples a DTC entrant to existing cellular bands: a bare handset can only tune the radios it physically has (band-specific front-end filters, PAs, a 3GPP modem), which cover cellular allocations ~600 MHz to ~6 GHz (coverage-grade DTC subset ~600 MHz-2.1 GHz); it has NO Ku/Ka/mmWave-satellite, L-band MSS, or any non-cellular radio. The antenna size does not unlock arbitrary spectrum. | `certified` | The phone radio set gates DTC spectrum | [direct_communication/dtc_spectrum_access.md](direct_communication/dtc_spectrum_access.md) Section 1.2; powerfulsignal, sqimway, FCC SCS R&O, cross-ref COMM-110, COMM-313 | FACT. |
| `COMM-483` | Answer to "can you use any spectrum": NO. A DTC satellite must transmit in a cellular band the phone's chipset already supports; the FCC SCS Report and Order authorizes exactly that set, and nothing outside it works to a bare phone, regardless of aperture. | `certified` | Cannot use arbitrary spectrum | [direct_communication/dtc_spectrum_access.md](direct_communication/dtc_spectrum_access.md) Section 1.2-1.3; FCC SCS R&O, Inside Global Tech SCS, cross-ref COMM-313 | FACT. |
| `COMM-484` | The FCC SCS Report and Order authorizes satellite-to-unmodified-phone operation in: 600 MHz (614-652 / 663-698), 700 MHz (698-769, 775-799, 805-806; incl. FirstNet Band 14), 800 MHz (824-849 / 869-894), Broadband PCS (1850-1915 / 1930-1995; incl. G-block), and AWS H-block (1915-1920 / 1995-2000). ### DTC data rate vs spectrum (COMM-493..512; COMM-513..520 reserved-unused)

| Claim ID | Claim text | Source status | Role | Links or internal references | Uncertainty notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `COMM-493` | The single-phone PEAK rate for a flat ~25 m^2 array at ~400 km on ~25 MHz owned spectrum is ~25-50 Mbps, well-supported and efficiency-driven: ~15-20 Mbps at Starlink-measured ~0.6 bps/Hz, ~50-75 Mbps at AST-claimed ~3 bps/Hz; the band assumes the array achieves better-than-Starlink efficiency, the load-bearing assumption. | `sourced_estimate` | The 25 MHz single-phone operating point | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); AST BW3 (Fierce, RCR), AST Block 1 (TelecomTalk), arXiv 2506.00283, cross-ref COMM-355/375 | FACT band; DERIVED central. |
| `COMM-494` | DTC single-device demonstration datapoints (triangulation set): AST BW3 ~10/14/21 Mbps (~64 m^2, thin channel), AST Block 1 ~98.9 Mbps, AST Block 2 ~200 Mbps device design, Starlink measured ~3.1 Mbps/beam (~25 m^2-class, 2x5 MHz), Starlink target "up to 100 Mbps" peak; Lynk/Globalstar/Huawei are narrowband with no published single-phone Mbps. | `certified` | The single-device datapoint set | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); Fierce, RCR, TelecomTalk, TechTimes, BusinessWire, arXiv 2506.00283, NextBigFuture | FACT for AST/Starlink; UNKNOWN/single-source for narrowband. |
| `COMM-495` | The sustained per-USER rate under load is confirmed multi-source: ~20-30 Mbps at 2-4 active users per cell (light), falling to ~500 kbps-1 Mbps when a cell carries hundreds of active phones; the analyst (Farrar/WIA), the academic worked example (Rappaport: 120 Mbps / 240 active = 500 kbps), and Starlink's own beam-sharing math (2-100 Mbps/user at 10-100 users/beam) all agree. | `certified` | The sustained-per-user rate under load | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); WIA/TMF, arXiv 2506.18672, arXiv 2506.00283, cross-ref COMM-417/418 | FACT, multi-source. |
| `COMM-496` | AST's "120 Mbps per cell" = 40 MHz x 3 bps/Hz is confirmed from 2+ independent sources (AST official + Rappaport arXiv); the 25 MHz x efficiency table is ~13-15 Mbps at 0.5-0.6 (Starlink measured), ~37.5 at 1.5, 50 at 2, 75 at 3 bps/Hz; the efficiency assumption is the entire swing. | `certified` | The AST 120 Mbps/cell confirmation | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); AST Next-Gen BlueBird, arXiv 2506.18672, arXiv 2506.00283, cross-ref COMM-344/430/431 | FACT + DERIVED. |
| `COMM-497` | FRAMING NUANCE (reconciled): AST Block 2 is quoted as 120 Mbps PER CELL (shared capacity, 40 MHz x 3) and ~200 Mbps PER DEVICE (best-case single-device peak); the corpus's use of 120 Mbps/cell as the capacity number is the correct conservative choice, and ~200 Mbps is a device peak, not cell capacity. | `certified` | Reconciles AST 120/cell vs 200/device | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); AST Next-Gen BlueBird, BusinessWire / TechTimes, cross-ref COMM-342 | FACT, reconciled. |
| `COMM-498` | THE CORE PHYSICS: for a fixed received power P, SNR = P / (N0 x B) is INVERSELY proportional to bandwidth, so widening the channel at fixed power lowers the power-spectral-density, lowers SNR-per-Hz, and lowers spectral efficiency. | `certified` | The fixed-power SNR-vs-bandwidth law | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); arXiv 0812.1553, ScienceDirect, cross-ref COMM-427 | FACT, multi-source. |
| `COMM-499` | Radio links split into two regimes (boundary ~2 bps/Hz): BANDWIDTH-limited (high SNR, efficiency >> 1) where rate is ~linear in bandwidth, and POWER-limited (low SNR, efficiency << 1) where capacity is linear in power and nearly INDEPENDENT of bandwidth. | `certified` | The two-regime split | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); Forney MIT OCW 6.451 Ch.4, Wikipedia Shannon-Hartley, Tse and Viswanath Ch.5 | FACT, textbook. |
| `COMM-500` | In the power-limited regime, as bandwidth grows at fixed power the capacity converges to a hard ceiling set ENTIRELY by power: C_infinity = (P/N0) x log2(e) = 1.44 x (P/N0), the wideband Shannon limit; the ultimate Eb/N0 floor is ln2 = -1.59 dB. Forney's deep-space example: "increasing P/N0 by 3 dB will double the achievable rate," i.e. rate tracks POWER not bandwidth once power-limited. | `certified` | The wideband power ceiling | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); Wikipedia Shannon-Hartley, dsplog, Forney Ch.4, Eb/N0 Wikipedia | FACT, textbook. |
| `COMM-501` | A DTC-to-phone link is POWER-LIMITED near-maximally: it sits at a MEASURED median SINR of 0 dB (SNR ~ 1) in Starlink's live network, explicitly "coverage-limited"; NTN links "operate close to power-amplifier saturation" due to the "very small link budget"; at ~0 dB the 3GPP NR MCS table pins the link to low-order QPSK (~0.5-0.8 bps/Hz) with no headroom. So the wideband-spreading penalty applies near-maximally. | `certified` | DTC is power-limited near-maximally | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); arXiv 2506.00283, 3GPP TR 38.821, Ericsson, WirelessBrew MCS, cross-ref COMM-294/429 | FACT, multi-source. |
| `COMM-502` | THE RATE-vs-BANDWIDTH FORK: rate is LINEAR in owned bandwidth ONLY in Case A (power grown proportionally to bandwidth to hold the power-spectral-density); at FIXED power (Case B) the rate SATURATES, because a DTC-to-phone link is power-limited. The corpus's "linear in bandwidth at constant efficiency" (COMM-374/430) is the OPTIMISTIC Case A and must carry the power caveat. | `derived_estimate` | The rate-vs-bandwidth fork | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md) Section 2; COMM-498..501, corpus COMM-348/374/378 | DERIVED + FACT. |
| `COMM-503` | CASE A (constant efficiency, power scaled with bandwidth) single-phone rate, R = B x efficiency at 2-3 bps/Hz: 25 MHz -> 50-75 Mbps, 50 MHz -> 100-150, 100 MHz -> 200-300, 200 MHz -> 400-600 Mbps; valid ONLY while the array can raise EIRP ~proportionally to B. | `derived_estimate` | The Case A (power-scaled) rate table | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md) Section 2.2; cross-ref COMM-374 | DERIVED. |
| `COMM-504` | CASE B (fixed power) single-phone rate, anchoring SNR = 1 (0 dB) at 25 MHz: efficiency falls ~half per doubling (50 MHz 0.585, 100 MHz 0.322, 200 MHz 0.170 bps/Hz), so rate is 25 MHz ~25 Mbps, 50 MHz ~29, 100 MHz ~32, 200 MHz ~34 Mbps, asymptoting to exactly 1.443x = log2(e) the 25 MHz rate; 8x the spectrum buys only ~1.36x the rate to one phone, hard-capped at ~1.44x. | `derived_estimate` | The Case B (fixed-power) saturation table | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md) Section 2.2; Forney Ch.4, Tse and Viswanath Ch.5, COMM-500 | DERIVED, arithmetic verified. |
| `COMM-505` | The fixed-power saturation bites the rate to ONE phone on a wide channel; it does NOT make wide spectrum worthless, because (a) carrier aggregation lets each added channel carry ANOTHER power-limited user at the same low efficiency, so the per-CELL aggregate grows with bandwidth (AST: "expanded bandwidth is the most viable path"), and (b) frequency-selective scheduling raises effective efficiency over a wide channel but needs multiple users and a frequency-selective channel, so it largely vanishes for a single user on a near-line-of-sight LEO link. | `certified` | Wide spectrum still adds aggregate capacity | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); Ericsson, Inseego, arXiv 2506.18672, arXiv 1201.6282, arXiv 1112.6117 | FACT, with limiter. |
| `COMM-506` | THE POWER RULE: to hold spectral efficiency constant as bandwidth grows 25 -> 200 MHz, the satellite must raise total transmit power PROPORTIONALLY to B (8x the bandwidth needs ~8x the EIRP, all else equal); power, not bandwidth, is the rate lever once the link is power-limited. | `certified` | The power rule | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md) Section 3.1; Forney Ch.4, COMM-498/500 | FACT, direct consequence of SNR = P/(N0 x B). |
| `COMM-507` | AST BlueBird power anchor (FCC technical narrative): total satellite RF power = 1,660 W (32.2 dBW), per-beam 10 dBW (10 W) x 160 beams; ~30 m^2 of silicon solar (~5-9 kW generation, NOT the single-tweet 100-120 kW); ~199 m^2 array (marketing ~223 m^2); RF power ~8.3 W/m^2 of array; downlink EIRP density ~+44 to +56 dBW/MHz per beam; designs to -124.5 dBW/m^2/MHz OOBE. | `certified` | The AST power/array anchor | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); FCC ELS id=376295 | FACT, FCC; DERIVED for per-m^2 and solar watts. |
| `COMM-508` | Starlink Direct-to-Cell power anchor (FCC technical narrative): ~25 m^2-class array, peak EIRP 58 dBW, peak EIRP density +57.7 dBW/MHz, peak gain 38 dBi; MEASURED median SINR to a phone = 0 dB over a ~5 MHz channel; bus/transmit power to the D2C payload is UNKNOWN (not in the filings); per-beam ~3.1 Mbps on 5 MHz = ~0.6 bps/Hz measured. | `certified` | The Starlink D2C power anchor | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); FCC IBFS SAT-MOD-20230207-00021, arXiv 2506.00283 | FACT for EIRP/gain; UNKNOWN for bus power. |
| `COMM-509` | WHERE POWER BINDS (the answer): anchored to Starlink delivering SNR ~ 1 over ~5 MHz from a ~25 m^2 array at +57.7 dBW/MHz, a flat ~25 m^2 entrant is Starlink-class (not AST-class) in EIRP density, so holding ~1-2 bps/Hz needs ~5-10x Starlink's per-beam power at 25 MHz, ~20x at 100 MHz, ~40x at 200 MHz; its fixed power is spread below ~1-2 bps/Hz in the ~50-100 MHz region. The model is BANDWIDTH-limited at ~25-50 MHz and flips to POWER-limited past ~50-100 MHz. | `sourced_estimate` | Where power binds (load-bearing) | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md) Section 3.3; anchors COMM-507/508, COMM-500/506 | ESTIMATE, load-bearing. |
| `COMM-510` | The exact knee bandwidth is bounded (~50-100 MHz) but NOT pinned, gated by the entrant's total satellite power (UNKNOWN, Flatellite power unpublished COMM-330) and Starlink's undisclosed D2C bus power; the knee slides toward ~100 MHz with an AST-class budget and toward ~25-50 MHz with a Starlink-class budget. What is firm: the binding limit FLIPS from bandwidth to power in that band, and at fixed power the single-phone rate cannot exceed ~1.44x the 25 MHz rate regardless of spectrum acquired. | `placeholder` | The knee is bounded but not pinned | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md) Section 3.3; cross-ref COMM-330, COMM-500/504/509 | UNKNOWN on the knee; FACT on the flip and ceiling. |
| `COMM-511` | The regulatory PFD cap does NOT bind the in-band downlink rate first: the FCC SCS Report and Order (FCC 24-28) explicitly DECLINED in-market in-band downlink PFD limits (in-band power set by the lease/partner, not a rule), capping only OOBE leakage at -120 dBW/m^2/MHz (SpaceX won a waiver to -110.6) and border field strength; the binding wall on in-band PSD is the array's own radiated power. | `certified` | The PFD cap does not bind in-band first | [direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md](direct_communication/dtc_data_rate_vs_spectrum.md); FCC 24-28, FCC DA-25-197 | FACT. |
| `COMM-512` | Net rate-vs-spectrum picture for the Neutron DTC model: the 25 MHz operating point (~25-50 Mbps single-phone, ~20-30 Mbps per-user light) is confirmed; the bandwidth dial is linear (Case A, to ~400-600 Mbps at 200 MHz) ONLY if power grows with it, and saturates at fixed power (Case B, ~34 Mbps at 200 MHz, capped at ~1.44x); power becomes the binding limit past ~50-100 MHz; wide spectrum retains value as AGGREGATE (more-users) capacity not single-phone peak; and the PFD cap does not bind in-band first. ## Claims To Repair Before Quoting Externally

The following wordings are specifically unsafe if quoted without context:

- "validated against two independent sources" for Rocket Lab LEO payload numbers
  when one source is Rocket Lab and the second merely repeats Rocket Lab.
- "Neutron SSO payload is 9.5 t" without `sourced_estimate` or "working
  figure."
- "`$10-20M` internal launch cost" as a single fact instead of a
  cadence-specific estimate range.
- "GB300 rack mass is `1,360 kg`" as a fixed value.
- "Vera Rubin NVL144" as the standard rack name.
- "Rubin CPX is `370 kW`" without a public source.
- "`$6M` GB300 rack cost is verified" without source-date qualification.
- "`$15-20B/GW-year`" or "`$25-50B/GW-year`" without distinguishing gross IaaS
  planning math from inference-service scenarios.
- "5-year GPU service life" as a confirmed field life rather than a design
  target.
- "The default proves orbital tokens are at parity with ground tokens." The
  current default implies about a `90%` token premium. The ground research basis
  is documented in this ledger and the promoted ground JSON now carries
  per-input source fields.
- "The 50 percent premium is the default." It is a solar-plus-radiator
  cost-down sensitivity, not the promoted default.
- "Future GPUs can safely run `20 deg C` hotter for five years" without
  vendor reliability data and explicit derating/redundancy assumptions.
- "`80 deg C` radiator service means GPU/HBM junctions can run `80 deg C` or
  hotter without a reliability penalty."
- "`1-2` day burn-in catches `90%` of defects."
- "`~40 MW/year` versus `~100 GW`" framed as a market-share thesis rather than
  a scale sanity check.

Communications wave 1 (2026-06-11):

- "The space-comms TAM is `$1.6T`" (or `$1.1T`) quoted as a reachable market.
  These are cited population-times-spend ceilings; the realistically served
  market is ~5-10% of them (`COMM-035`, `COMM-036`, `COMM-038`). Always state the
  haircut.
- "US wireless service revenue is `$326B`" as a hard fact. It is a single-major-
  source (IBISWorld) figure; cite it as `sourced_estimate` (`COMM-009`).
- "Global telecom is a `$7.6 trillion` market." The `$7.6T` is GDP contribution,
  not operator revenue, and must never be summed with market-size lines; the
  collected revenue is ~$1.19T mobile (`COMM-019`).
- "`$2.34/Mbps` to `$0.02/Mbps`" or "`90%` decline gigabit" quoted as a forecast.
  These are a peer-reviewed WTP study and revealed behavior (`COMM-005`,
  `COMM-006`), evidence of a plateau, not a projection.
- "Terrestrial laser replaces fiber" as a general claim. It is a gap-filler;
  alongside lit fiber its value is conditional (security, latency, fast deploy),
  and the DC-interconnect use is nascent/single-vendor (`COMM-050`).
- "FSO is unbreakable / un-tappable." It is physical-layer obscurity plus
  tamper-evidence, not cryptographic security; sensitive traffic still needs
  encryption (`COMM-050`).
- "Starship will run orbital data centers in `~3 years`." The Falcon 9 analogue
  supports "at least about 5 years" as the floor; the ~3-year claim is a best-case
  ceiling, not a base case (`COMM-053`).
- Verizon and AT&T market caps and FY2025 revenue/net income differ between the
  two US market docs (different aggregator timestamps): Verizon cap ~$191B vs
  ~$201B, AT&T cap ~$156B vs ~$160B, AT&T revenue $125.7B vs $125.6B, Verizon NI
  $17.2B vs $17.6B (`COMM-003`, `COMM-011`). Quote a single reconciled value, not
  both, and date it.

Communications wave 2 (2026-06-11):

- The wave-2 dollar bands are `[ILLUSTRATIVE]` (mapped to `scenario`), not
  forecasts: the rural fringe ~$40-55B to ~$95-130B/yr (`COMM-057`, `COMM-058`),
  the consolidated new-entrant-addressable ~$45-60B to ~$110-150B/yr (`COMM-075`),
  and the premium open slice ~$8-30B/yr (`COMM-071`). Quote them as sized
  demand-side pools with the conservative-to-mid band as the center of gravity,
  never as expected revenue, and always ex-China.
- "The premium/sovereign niche is a `$60-95B/yr` market" quoted as addressable.
  That is the GROSS spend pool (`COMM-070`, reconciled DOWN from the prior ~$75-95B
  headline); only ~$8-30B/yr is open to a new commercial entrant (`COMM-071`). The cited flagship programs (IRIS2 EUR 10.6B,
  the SDA tranches, the $2.29B SpaceX SDN, GOVSATCOM) are CLOSED prime/consortium
  builds, demand proof not contestable revenue (`COMM-067`); counting them as
  addressable repeats the error the base warns against.
- The wave-2 pools are the all-operators contestable pie, not one operator's
  capture. Any single-entrant revenue is a SHARE of `COMM-075`, further gated by
  the supply-side capacity limit and the unproven space-supply economics, none of
  which these demand-side numbers assume.
- "Add the rural fringe and the premium niche together." They OVERLAP on the
  mobility/enterprise verticals (`COMM-078`); they must be de-duplicated (the
  consolidation's five-bucket decomposition), not summed.
- The premium/sovereign gross-pool roll-up (`COMM-070`) is stated as ~$60-95B/yr
  (lead-reconciled 2026-06-11), because its own component lines sum nearer the low
  end (~$60-70B); treat the band as a wide-component-range ceiling, and prefer
  citing the components or the ~$60-95B span, never a tight ~$75-95B headline.
- The carrier direct-to-cell add-on (~$20-30B of the optimistic case) is a bolt-on
  across the general mobile base, not rural-fringe broadband; whether it belongs in
  a space-comms addressable number is an open definitional call (`COMM-059`,
  `COMM-079`). Stripping it gives a rural-plus-premium-proper optimistic ceiling of
  ~$90-120B/yr.

## Maintenance Rules

When a research doc introduces a new hard number:

1. Add a stable claim ID here.
2. Mark it with one of the exact source statuses:
   `certified`, `sourced_estimate`, `derived_estimate`, `projection`,
   `extrapolation`, `scenario`, `placeholder`, or `stale`.
3. Cite sources inline in the topic doc and in this ledger.
4. State whether the claim is a model input, model output, public doc claim, or
   open research question.
5. Add uncertainty notes.
6. If the value is model-generated, link the model path or generated output, but
   keep the status as `derived_estimate` unless an external source verifies the
   final claim.
7. Update [LIBRARY.md](LIBRARY.md) and [RESEARCH_TRACKER.md](RESEARCH_TRACKER.md).
